Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

356: +// assertAttributeParity checks that the c9s event carries every attribute of the Docker event,
357: +// apart from the ones listed.
358: +func assertAttributeParity(t *testing.T, docker, c9s aggregatedEvent, dockerOnly map[string]bool) {
359: +	t.Helper()
360: +
361: +	dockerAttributes := mergedEventAttributes(docker)
362: +	c9sAttributes := mergedEventAttributes(c9s)
363: +	var missing []string
364: +	for key := range dockerAttributes {
365: +		if _, ok := c9sAttributes[key]; !ok && !dockerOnly[key] {
366: +			missing = append(missing, key)
367: +		}
368: +	}
369: +	if len(missing) != 0 {
370: +		sort.Strings(missing)
371: +		t.Fatalf("c9s %s %s lacks %v\ndocker: %v\nc9s:    %v", c9s.Type, c9s.Action, missing,
372: +			attributeKeys(dockerAttributes), attributeKeys(c9sAttributes))
373: +	}
374: +}

377: +// lab read with its topology file (events -t) and without it (events --name), where the
378: +// attributes come from the cluster alone.
379: +func TestContainerEventParity(t *testing.T) {

382: +	if err != nil {
383: +		t.Fatal(err)
384: +	}

387: +		name string

389: +	}{{"with the topology", dockerLab}, {"by name", byName}} {
390: +		t.Run(mode.name, func(t *testing.T) {
391: +			assertContainerEventParity(t, path, dockerLab, mode.lab, mode.lab == byName)
392: +		})
393: +	}
394: +}

396: +func assertContainerEventParity(
397: +	t *testing.T,
398: +	path string,
399: +	dockerLab, c9sLab *clabcore.CLab,
400: +	byName bool,
401: +) {
402: +	t.Helper()
403: +
404: +	for _, node := range []string{"client", "srl1"} {
405: +		container := dockerContainer(t, dockerLab, node)
406: +		state, nodeState := c9sNode(dockerLab, path, node)
407: +		dockerOnly := dockerOnlyContainerAttributes
408: +		if byName && node == "srl1" {
409: +			// The type is the SR Linux default; the cluster has only the types a topology
410: +			// sets, so a lab read by name does not know it.
411: +			dockerOnly = map[string]bool{clabconstants.NodeType: true}
412: +			for key := range dockerOnlyContainerAttributes {
413: +				dockerOnly[key] = true
414: +			}
415: +		}
416: +
417: +		for _, tt := range []struct {
418: +			action, state string
419: +			extra         map[string]string
420: +		}{
421: +			{"create", "created", nil},
422: +			{"start", "running", nil},
423: +			{"kill", "running", map[string]string{"signal": "15"}},
424: +			{"stop", "exited", nil},
425: +			{"die", "exited", map[string]string{"exitCode": "137", "execDuration": "3"}},
426: +			{"health_status: healthy", "running", nil},
427: +			{"destroy", "exited", nil},
428: +		} {
429: +			docker := dockerContainerEvent(t, container, tt.action, tt.extra)
430: +			var c9sExtra map[string]string
431: +			if tt.action == "kill" {
432: +				c9sExtra = tt.extra
433: +			}
434: +			c9s := c9sContainerEvent(c9sLab, state, nodeState, tt.action, tt.state, c9sExtra)
435: +			assertAttributeParity(t, docker, c9s, dockerOnly)
436: +
437: +			got := mergedEventAttributes(c9s)
438: +			want := map[string]string{
439: +				clabconstants.Containerlab: "parity",
440: +				clabconstants.NodeName:     node,
441: +				clabconstants.LongName:     "parity-" + node,
442: +				clabconstants.NodeKind:     dockerLab.Nodes[node].Config().Kind,
443: +				clabconstants.TopoFile:     path,
444: +				clabconstants.Owner:        "alice",
445: +				"image":                    container.Image,
446: +				"name":                     "parity-" + node,
447: +				"id":                       "c9s-parity/parity/" + node,
448: +				"mgmt_ipv4":                "172.20.20.84/24",
449: +				"mgmt_ipv6":                "3fff:172:20:20::84/64",
450: +				"ports":                    "22/tcp;80/tcp",
451: +				"state":                    tt.state,
452: +			}
453: +			for key, value := range want {
454: +				if got[key] != value {
455: +					t.Fatalf("%s %s: %s = %q, want %q", node, tt.action, key, got[key], value)
456: +				}
457: +			}
458: +			// the label values have Docker's format
459: +			dockerAttributes := mergedEventAttributes(docker)
460: +			for _, key := range []string{
461: +				clabconstants.Containerlab, clabconstants.NodeName, clabconstants.NodeKind,
462: +				clabconstants.NodeType, clabconstants.TopoFile, clabconstants.Owner, "image",
463: +				"ports",
464: +			} {
465: +				if dockerOnly[key] {
466: +					continue
467: +				}
468: +				if got[key] != dockerAttributes[key] {
469: +					t.Fatalf("%s %s: %s = %q, docker %q", node, tt.action, key, got[key],
470: +						dockerAttributes[key])
471: +				}
472: +			}
473: +			if c9s.ActorID != c9s.ActorFullID || c9s.ActorName != "parity-"+node {
474: +				t.Fatalf("%s %s: actor = %+v", node, tt.action, c9s)
475: +			}
476: +		}
477: +	}
478: +}

505: +// TestContainerStateUsesDockerVocabulary checks the state and status of every node state c9s
506: +// reports, in events and inspect.
507: +func TestContainerStateUsesDockerVocabulary(t *testing.T) {
508: +	t.Parallel()
509: +
510: +	c9sLab, err := clabcore.NewContainerLab(clabcore.WithLabNameOnly("parity"))
511: +	if err != nil {
512: +		t.Fatal(err)
513: +	}

519: +	}{

522: +			"created", "Created",
523: +		},
524: +		{clablabruntime.NodeState{
525: +			Name: "b", ContainerState: "running", Health: "starting",
526: +			State: "notready",
527: +		}, "running", "health: starting"},
528: +		{clablabruntime.NodeState{
529: +			Name: "c", ContainerState: "running", Health: "healthy",
530: +			State: "ready", Ready: true,
531: +		}, "running", "healthy"},
532: +		{
533: +			clablabruntime.NodeState{Name: "d", ContainerState: "exited", State: "stopped"},
534: +			"exited", "Exited",
535: +		},
536: +	}
537: +	for _, tt := range tests {
538: +		container := c9sLab.LabRuntimeContainer(state, tt.node)
539: +		if container.State != tt.state || container.Status != tt.status {
540: +			t.Fatalf("%s: %s / %s, want %s / %s", tt.node.Name, container.State, container.Status,
541: +				tt.state, tt.status)
542: +		}
543: +	}
544: +}

546: +// dockerInterfaceEvent is a netlink interface event of a Docker container.
547: +func dockerInterfaceEvent(
548: +	container clabruntime.GenericContainer,
549: +	action string,
550: +	snapshot ifaceSnapshot,
551: +	stats *ifaceStatsMetrics,
552: +) aggregatedEvent {
553: +	attributes := interfaceAttributes(&container, snapshot)
554: +	if stats != nil {
555: +		interfaceStatsAttributes(attributes, *stats)
556: +	}
557: +
558: +	return aggregatedEvent{
559: +		Type: "interface", Action: action, ActorID: container.ShortID,
560: +		ActorName: container.Names[0], ActorFullID: container.ID, Attributes: attributes,
561: +	}
562: +}

564: +func c9sInterfaceEvent(
565: +	state *clablabruntime.LabState,
566: +	node clablabruntime.NodeState,
567: +	action string,
568: +	iface clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData,
569: +) aggregatedEvent {
570: +	key := state.Namespace + "/" + state.Name + "/" + node.Name
571: +
572: +	return aggregatedEventFromLabRuntimeEvent(nil, clablabruntime.Event{
573: +		Type: clablabruntime.EventTypeInterface, Action: action,
574: +		ActorID: key, ActorName: state.Name + "-" + node.Name, ActorFullID: key,
575: +		Attributes: map[string]string{
576: +			"namespace": state.Namespace, "lab": state.Name, "node": node.Name, "pod": node.Pod,
577: +			"origin": "clabernetes",
578: +		},
579: +		Lab: state, Node: &node, Interface: &iface,
580: +	})
581: +}

583: +// TestInterfaceEventParity compares the interface events of the lab link (linux eth1, SR Linux
584: +// e1-1 with its alias ethernet-1/1), the management interface and loopback.
585: +func TestInterfaceEventParity(t *testing.T) {
586: +	path, dockerLab := parityLab(t)
587: +
588: +	tests := []struct {
589: +		node string
590: +		data clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData
591: +	}{
592: +		{
593: +			"client",
594: +			clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData{
595: +				Name:      "eth1",
596: +				Index:     7,
597: +				MTU:       9500,
598: +				MAC:       "aa:c1:ab:56:aa:46",
599: +				OperState: "up",
600: +				Type:      "veth",
601: +			},
602: +		},
603: +		{"srl1", clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData{
604: +			Name: "e1-1", Alias: "ethernet-1/1", Index: 7, MTU: 9500, MAC: "aa:c1:ab:56:aa:47",
605: +			OperState: "lower-layer-down", Type: "veth",
606: +		}},
607: +		{
608: +			"client",
609: +			clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData{
610: +				Name:      "eth0",
611: +				Index:     4,
612: +				MTU:       1500,
613: +				MAC:       "02:42:ac:14:14:02",
614: +				OperState: "up",
615: +				Type:      "veth",
616: +			},
617: +		},
618: +		{"client", clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData{
619: +			Name: "lo", Index: 1, MTU: 65536, OperState: "unknown", Type: "device",
620: +		}},
621: +	}
622: +	for _, tt := range tests {
623: +		container := dockerContainer(t, dockerLab, tt.node)
624: +		state, nodeState := c9sNode(dockerLab, path, tt.node)
625: +		snapshot := ifaceSnapshot{
626: +			Index: tt.data.Index, Name: tt.data.Name, Alias: tt.data.Alias, MTU: tt.data.MTU,
627: +			MAC: tt.data.MAC, OperState: tt.data.OperState, Type: tt.data.Type,
628: +		}
629: +		metrics := ifaceStatsMetrics{
630: +			RxBytes: 2000, TxBytes: 3000, RxPackets: 20, TxPackets: 30,
631: +			RxBps: 8000, TxBps: 12000, RxPps: 10, TxPps: 15, Interval: 2 * time.Second,
632: +		}
633: +
634: +		for _, action := range []string{"snapshot", "create", "update", "delete", "stats"} {
635: +			var stats *ifaceStatsMetrics
636: +			data := tt.data
637: +			if action == "stats" {
638: +				stats = &metrics
639: +				data.Stats = &clablabruntime.InterfaceStats{
640: +					RxBytes: 2000, TxBytes: 3000, RxPackets: 20, TxPackets: 30,
641: +					RxBps: 8000, TxBps: 12000, RxPps: 10, TxPps: 15, Interval: 2 * time.Second,
642: +				}
643: +			}
644: +			docker := dockerInterfaceEvent(container, action, snapshot, stats)
645: +			c9s := c9sInterfaceEvent(state, nodeState, action, data)
646: +			assertAttributeParity(t, docker, c9s, nil)
647: +
648: +			dockerAttributes := mergedEventAttributes(docker)
649: +			got := mergedEventAttributes(c9s)
650: +			for key, value := range dockerAttributes {
651: +				switch key {
652: +				case "id", "name", "lab", "origin":
653: +					// the node's own ID, name and lab; the source of the event
654: +					continue
655: +				}
656: +				if got[key] != value {
657: +					t.Fatalf("%s %s %s: %s = %q, docker %q", tt.node, tt.data.Name, action, key,
658: +						got[key], value)
659: +				}
660: +			}
661: +			if got["name"] != "parity-"+tt.node || got["lab"] != "parity" ||
662: +				got["id"] != "c9s-parity/parity/"+tt.node {
663: +				t.Fatalf("%s %s %s: node attributes %v", tt.node, tt.data.Name, action, got)
664: +			}
665: +		}
666: +	}
667: +}

685: +	}

688: +	}

729: +// snapshot like the snapshots of local containers, otherwise with the labels, image,

740:  	}

749: +		} else {

751: +		}

753: +		lab := ev.Attributes["lab"]

763: +		}
764: +		attributes[key] = value
765: +	}

963:  
964: +// labRuntimeContainerState is a lab runtime node's state and status in the vocabulary of the
965: +// container runtimes: state created, running or exited; status healthy or "health: starting"
966: +// for a running node, as inspect shortens a Docker status line with a healthcheck, Created or
967: +// Exited otherwise. A runtime that reports no container state keeps its own state.
968: +func labRuntimeContainerState(
969: +	state *clablabruntime.LabState,
970: +	node clablabruntime.NodeState,
971: +) (string, string) {
972: +	switch node.ContainerState {
973: +	case clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning:
974: +		if node.Health == clablabruntime.ContainerHealthHealthy {
975: +			return node.ContainerState, "healthy"
976: +		}
977: +
978: +		return node.ContainerState, "health: starting"
979: +	case clablabruntime.ContainerStateCreated:
980: +		return node.ContainerState, "Created"
981: +	case clablabruntime.ContainerStateExited:
982: +		return node.ContainerState, "Exited"
983: +	}
984: +
985: +	if node.Ready {
986: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning, "healthy"
987: +	}
988: +	if node.State == "" {
989: +		return state.State, ""
990: +	}
991: +
992: +	return node.State, node.State
993: +}
994: +
995: +// labRuntimePorts reports the ports a lab runtime exposes for a node as the ports a container
996: +// exposes without publishing them on the host: the node is reached through the runtime (a
997: +// Service, a port-forward), never on a port of this machine.
998: +func labRuntimePorts(ports []clablabruntime.ExposedPort) []*clabtypes.GenericPortBinding {
999: +	if len(ports) == 0 {
1000: +		return nil
1001: +	}
1002: +
1003: +	bindings := make([]*clabtypes.GenericPortBinding, 0, len(ports))
1004: +	for _, port := range ports {
1005: +		bindings = append(bindings, &clabtypes.GenericPortBinding{
1006: +			ContainerPort: port.Port,
1007: +			Protocol:      port.Protocol,
1008: +		})
1009: +	}
1010: +
1011: +	return bindings
1012: +}
1013: +
1014:  // labRuntimeContainerID is the ID of a lab runtime node: <namespace>/<lab>/<node>.
1015:  func labRuntimeContainerID(namespace, lab, node string) string {
1016:  	return fmt.Sprintf("%s/%s/%s", namespace, lab, node)
1017:  }

1117:  
1118:  The `inspect interfaces` subcommand provides information about the network interfaces of deployed nodes of a deployed lab.
1119:  The subcommand gathers information directly from deployed containers, and displays information about their operational state, network interface type, and if applicable, the applied network interface alias.

1122: +With the [c9s runtime](../../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#interface-events), the listing covers the interfaces a Docker container of the node has: the lab link interfaces of each running node (the ones [`capture`](../capture.md) accepts) with the alias the topology used, the management interface `eth0` and the loopback `lo`, with the MAC address, index, MTU and operational state read in the node's device container. 1148: +### Topology file
1149: +
1150: +A deploy records the absolute path of the topology file, on the machine that
1151: +deployed it, in the annotation `containerlab.dev/topo-file`:
1152: +
1153: +- with a `Topology` resource, on the `Topology`; every deploy records the file
1154: +  it read
1155: +- with `--no-topology-cr`, on every `Node`. clabernetes copies a Node's
1156: +  annotations into its pod, so a Node keeps the file it was created from: a
1157: +  later deploy from another file does not change it and does not restart the
1158: +  node. Destroy and deploy again to record a new file.
1159: +
1160: +`inspect` reports the recorded file as `labPath` and `absLabPath` (shortened
1161: +for the table, as for Docker), and events as `clab-topo-file`, so a client
1162: +matches a running lab to its file the same way for every runtime. The file is a
1163: +path on the deploying machine; a client on another machine cannot assume it
1164: +exists there.
1165: +
1166: +A lab that containerlab did not deploy from a file, for example one applied from
1167: +`deploy --emit-crs` output with `kubectl` (the emitted manifests do not carry
1168: +the annotation), has no recorded file. It reports where its definition lives in
1169: +the cluster: `k8s://<namespace>/topologies/<lab>`, or
1170: +`k8s://<namespace>/labs/<lab>` without a `Topology` resource. With `-t`,
1171: +`inspect` reports the given file.
1172: +
1173:  Useful kubernetes checks for the same state are:
1174:  
1175:  ```bash
1176:  kubectl -n <namespace> get node.c9s.run,link.c9s.run,nodeprofile.c9s.run,deploy,pod,svc,cm,pvc \
1177:    -l c9s.run/topologyOwner=<lab>

1182:    configuration survives a stop and start.
1183:  - Its links go carrier-down on every peer, like an unplugged cable;
1184: -  `events` reports them as interface `down` events.
1185: -- `inspect` shows the node as `stopped` (`stopping` while its Pod terminates).
1186: +  `events` reports an interface `update` with state `lower-layer-down` on the
1187: +  peers, and deletes the stopped node's interfaces.
1188: +- `inspect` shows the node as `exited` with status `Exited`.
1189: +- `events` reports the container actions Docker reports for `containerlab stop`
1190: +  and `start`: `kill`, `stop` and `die` when the node's pod goes, `start` when
1191: +  its new pod runs, then `health_status: starting` and

1238: +### Container events
1239: +
1240: +Each node is reported as a container. Its state follows its pod: `created`
1241: +while the pod is scheduled and prepared, `running` once the device containers
1242: +run, `exited` once the node is stopped, or its pod ends or is replaced. A change
1243: +is reported with the actions Docker reports for the same change:
1244: +
1245: +| Change | Actions |
1246: +|---|---|
1247: +| the node appears (deploy, apply) | `create`, then `start` once its pod runs |
1248: +| the device boots, is ready, is not ready anymore | `health_status: starting`, `health_status: healthy`, `health_status: unhealthy` |
1249: +| `stop` | `kill`, `stop`, `die` |
1250: +| `start` | `start` |
1251: +| `restart`, or the pod is replaced | `kill`, `stop`, `die`, `start` |
1252: +| the node is removed (destroy, apply) | `kill`, `die` (when it ran), `destroy` |
1253: +
1254: +With `--initial-state`, the stream starts with one event per node whose action
1255: +is its state (`running`, `exited`, `created`) and `origin` `snapshot`, like the
1256: +snapshot of a Docker container.

1299: +  `exited`. Docker leaves it to the client to derive it from the action; c9s
1300: +  reports it on every container event.
1301: +- `ports` lists the node's exposed ports without a host binding: they are
1302: +  reached through the node's Service or a port-forward, not on a port of the
1303: +  local machine.
1304: +- `kill` carries `signal` `15`; Kubernetes stops containers with SIGTERM.
1305: +- `create` can come before the controller has allocated the node's management
1306: +  address and Service, so it may lack `mgmt_ipv4`, `mgmt_ipv6` and `ports`;
1307: +  the following events carry them.
1308: +- `namespace`, `lab`, `node` and `pod` are c9s attributes on top.
1309: +
1310: +Docker attributes c9s does not report: `scope`, `clab-mgmt-net-bridge` (no
1311: +Docker bridge), `clab-node-lab-dir` (a c9s lab has no lab directory on the
1312: +deploying machine; node state lives in the cluster), and `exitCode` and
1313: +`execDuration` on `die`. `clab-node-type` and `clab-node-group` are reported
1314: +when the topology sets them, and with `-t` also a kind's default type; read by
1315: +`--name` alone, a default type such as SR Linux's `ixr-d2l` is not known.
1316:  
1317:  ### Interface events
1318:  
1319:  Containerlab samples the interfaces of every running node by exec into its
1320:  device container. One exec per node and interval reads `/proc/net/dev` and
1321:  `/sys/class/net/*/operstate` (with index, MTU, MAC address, flags and alias).
1322: -All containers of a device pod share one network namespace, so the samples
1323: -cover the node's pod interfaces.
1324:  
1325: -Interface state events are always streamed. They use the same schema as the
1326: -interface events of the local runtimes, with the operational state as action:
1327: +It reports the interfaces a Docker container of the same node kind has, named
1328: +as in that container:
1329: +
1330: +- the node's lab link interfaces, with the names and aliases clabwire lists for
1331: +  them (`containerlab capture --list`): `eth1` for a linux node, `e1-1` with
1332: +  alias `ethernet-1/1` for SR Linux, so a client finds a link's interface by
1333: +  the endpoint name in the topology or its alias
1334: +- the management interface `eth0`
1335: +- the loopback `lo`
1336: +
1337: +The pod's own interfaces (the pod network interface `c9s0`, clabernetes'
1338: +interposition interfaces `c9sr0` and `c9sm0`, and the link transport interfaces
1339: +`c9ss*`) are not reported, and neither are the aliases clabernetes sets for its
1340: +own use (`c9s:…`).
1341: +
1342: +Interface state events are always streamed, with the actions and attributes of
1343: +the local runtimes' netlink events:

1729: +package clabernetes
1730: +
1731: +import (
1732: +	"context"
1733: +	"errors"
1734: +	"fmt"
1735: +	"strings"
1736: +	"time"

1741: +
1742: +// Container event actions, as the Docker runtime reports them.
1743: +const (
1744: +	containerActionCreate        = "create"
1745: +	containerActionStart         = "start"
1746: +	containerActionKill          = "kill"
1747: +	containerActionStop          = "stop"
1748: +	containerActionDie           = "die"
1749: +	containerActionDestroy       = "destroy"
1750: +	containerActionHealthPrefix  = "health_status: "
1751: +	containerEventSignalSIGTERM  = "15"
1752: +	containerRefreshDelay        = 200 * time.Millisecond
1753: +	containerRefreshRetryDelay   = pollInterval
1754: +	containerEventAttributeState = "state"
1755: +)
1756: +
1757: +// trackedContainer is the last reported container state of a node.
1758: +type trackedContainer struct {
1759: +	ref    labRef
1760: +	state  string
1761: +	health string
1762: +	pod    string
1763: +	// lab and node are the last observation, which a removed node is described with.
1764: +	lab  *clablabruntime.LabState
1765: +	node clablabruntime.NodeState
1766: +}
1767: +
1768: +// containerTracker turns the node states of the labs in an event scope into the container
1769: +// events the Docker runtime emits for the same lifecycle: create and start when a node's pod
1770: +// starts, kill, stop and die when it stops (on request, or between the pods of a restart),
1771: +// destroy when the node is removed, and health_status as its device becomes ready.
1772: +type containerTracker struct {
1773: +	r     *Runtime
1774: +	sink  chan<- clablabruntime.Event
1775: +	nodes map[string]*trackedContainer
1776: +}
1777: +
1778: +// trackContainers reports the container events of a scope. It reads the labs once, reporting
1779: +// them as a snapshot when the stream includes the initial state, and then reads a lab again
1780: +// whenever its Topology, Nodes or pods change. Changes that arrive together are read once.
1781: +func (r *Runtime) trackContainers(
1782: +	ctx context.Context,
1783: +	scope eventScope,
1784: +	includeInitialState bool,
1785: +	changed <-chan labRef,
1786: +	eventSink chan<- clablabruntime.Event,
1787: +	errSink chan<- error,
1788: +) {
1789: +	tracker := &containerTracker{r: r, sink: eventSink, nodes: map[string]*trackedContainer{}}
1790: +
1791: +	states, err := r.labStates(ctx, scope)
1792: +	if err != nil {
1793: +		sendEventError(ctx, errSink, err)
1794: +		return
1795: +	}
1796: +	for _, state := range states {
1797: +		tracker.observeLab(ctx, labRef{namespace: state.Namespace, lab: state.Name}, state,
1798: +			&observeSeed{snapshot: includeInitialState})
1799: +	}
1800: +
1801: +	pending := map[labRef]bool{}
1802: +	timer := time.NewTimer(time.Hour)
1803: +	timer.Stop()
1804: +	defer timer.Stop()
1805: +	armed := false
1806: +	arm := func(delay time.Duration) {
1807: +		if !armed {
1808: +			timer.Reset(delay)
1809: +			armed = true
1810: +		}
1811: +	}
1812: +
1813: +	for {
1814: +		select {
1815: +		case <-ctx.Done():
1816: +			return
1817: +		case ref := <-changed:
1818: +			pending[ref] = true
1819: +			arm(containerRefreshDelay)
1820: +		case <-timer.C:
1821: +			armed = false
1822: +			refs := pending
1823: +			pending = map[labRef]bool{}
1824: +			for ref := range refs {
1825: +				if !tracker.refresh(ctx, ref) {
1826: +					pending[ref] = true
1827: +				}
1828: +			}
1829: +			if len(pending) != 0 {
1830: +				arm(containerRefreshRetryDelay)
1831: +			}
1832: +		}
1833: +	}
1834: +}
1835: +
1836: +// observeSeed marks the first observation of a stream: it records the node states without
1837: +// reporting changes, and reports them as a snapshot when asked to.
1838: +type observeSeed struct {
1839: +	snapshot bool
1840: +}
1841: +
1842: +// refresh reads a lab again and reports what changed for its nodes. A lab that is gone reports
1843: +// its nodes as destroyed. It returns false when the lab could not be read.
1844: +func (t *containerTracker) refresh(ctx context.Context, ref labRef) bool {
1845: +	state, err := t.r.Inspect(ctx, clablabruntime.InspectRequest{
1846: +		Name:      ref.lab,
1847: +		Namespace: ref.namespace,
1848: +	})
1849: +	if err != nil {
1850: +		var notFound *labNotFoundError
1851: +		if !errors.As(err, &notFound) {
1852: +			if ctx.Err() == nil {
1853: +				log.Debug("Failed to read a C9s lab for container events",
1854: +					"namespace", ref.namespace, "lab", ref.lab, "error", err)
1855: +			}
1856: +
1857: +			return ctx.Err() != nil
1858: +		}
1859: +		state = nil
1860: +	}
1861: +
1862: +	t.observeLab(ctx, ref, state, nil)
1863: +
1864: +	return true
1865: +}
1866: +
1867: +// observeLab compares the nodes of a lab with what was reported before and reports the
1868: +// difference. A nil state removes every node of the lab.
1869: +func (t *containerTracker) observeLab(
1870: +	ctx context.Context,
1871: +	ref labRef,
1872: +	state *clablabruntime.LabState,
1873: +	seed *observeSeed,
1874: +) {
1875: +	seen := map[string]bool{}
1876: +	if state != nil {
1877: +		for idx := range state.Nodes {
1878: +			key := c9sNodeKey(state.Namespace, state.Name, state.Nodes[idx].Name)
1879: +			seen[key] = true
1880: +			t.observeNode(ctx, ref, state, state.Nodes[idx], seed)
1881: +		}
1882: +	}
1883: +
1884: +	for key, tracked := range t.nodes {
1885: +		if tracked.ref != ref || seen[key] {
1886: +			continue
1887: +		}
1888: +
1889: +		for _, action := range containerRemovalActions(tracked) {
1890: +			t.emit(ctx, tracked.lab, tracked.node, action, nil)
1891: +		}
1892: +		delete(t.nodes, key)
1893: +	}
1894: +}
1895: +
1896: +func (t *containerTracker) observeNode(
1897: +	ctx context.Context,
1898: +	ref labRef,
1899: +	state *clablabruntime.LabState,
1900: +	node clablabruntime.NodeState,
1901: +	seed *observeSeed,
1902: +) {
1903: +	key := c9sNodeKey(state.Namespace, state.Name, node.Name)
1904: +	previous := t.nodes[key]
1905: +	if node.ContainerState == "" {
1906: +		node.ContainerState = clablabruntime.ContainerStateCreated
1907: +	}
1908: +	next := &trackedContainer{
1909: +		ref:   ref,
1910: +		state: node.ContainerState,
1911: +		pod:   node.Pod,
1912: +		lab:   state,
1913: +		node:  node,
1914: +	}
1915: +	next.health = containerHealth(previous, next, node.Health)
1916: +	t.nodes[key] = next
1917: +
1918: +	if seed != nil {
1919: +		if seed.snapshot {
1920: +			t.emit(ctx, state, node, next.state, map[string]string{
1921: +				"origin": clablabruntime.EventOriginSnapshot,
1922: +			})
1923: +		}
1924: +
1925: +		return
1926: +	}
1927: +
1928: +	for _, action := range containerTransitionActions(previous, next) {
1929: +		t.emit(ctx, state, node, action, nil)
1930: +	}
1931: +}
1932: +
1933: +// containerHealth is the health a running node reports: a node that was healthy and is not
1934: +// ready anymore in the same pod is unhealthy, as a container whose healthcheck starts failing.
1935: +func containerHealth(previous, next *trackedContainer, health string) string {
1936: +	if next.state != clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning {
1937: +		return ""
1938: +	}
1939: +	if health == "" {
1940: +		health = clablabruntime.ContainerHealthStarting
1941: +	}
1942: +	if health == clablabruntime.ContainerHealthStarting && previous != nil &&
1943: +		previous.state == clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning && previous.pod == next.pod &&
1944: +		(previous.health == clablabruntime.ContainerHealthHealthy ||
1945: +			previous.health == clablabruntime.ContainerHealthUnhealthy) {
1946: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerHealthUnhealthy
1947: +	}
1948: +
1949: +	return health
1950: +}
1951: +
1952: +// containerTransitionActions are the container event actions Docker reports when a container
1953: +// goes from the previous to the next state:
1954: +//
1955: +//	new node            create (+ start when it runs, + stop when it is already stopped)
1956: +//	created -> running  start
1957: +//	exited  -> running  start
1958: +//	running -> exited   kill, stop, die (stop, or the old pod of a restart ending)
1959: +//	running -> created  kill, stop, die (the replacement pod of a restart not running yet)
1960: +//	running, new pod    kill, stop, die, start (restart)
1961: +//	created -> exited   stop
1962: +//
1963: +// A started node also reports its health (health_status: starting or healthy), and a running
1964: +// node reports health changes, like a container with a healthcheck.
1965: +func containerTransitionActions(previous, next *trackedContainer) []string {
1966: +	const (
1967: +		created = clablabruntime.ContainerStateCreated
1968: +		running = clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning
1969: +		exited  = clablabruntime.ContainerStateExited
1970: +	)
1971: +
1972: +	var actions []string
1973: +	previousState := ""
1974: +	if previous != nil {
1975: +		previousState = previous.state
1976: +	}
1977: +
1978: +	switch {
1979: +	case previous == nil:
1980: +		actions = append(actions, containerActionCreate)
1981: +		switch next.state {
1982: +		case running:
1983: +			actions = append(actions, containerActionStart)
1984: +		case exited:
1985: +			actions = append(actions, containerActionStop)
1986: +		}
1987: +	case previousState == running && next.state == running && previous.pod != next.pod:
1988: +		actions = append(actions, containerActionKill, containerActionStop, containerActionDie,
1989: +			containerActionStart)
1990: +	case previousState == running && next.state != running:
1991: +		actions = append(actions, containerActionKill, containerActionStop, containerActionDie)
1992: +	case previousState != running && next.state == running:
1993: +		actions = append(actions, containerActionStart)
1994: +	case previousState == created && next.state == exited:
1995: +		actions = append(actions, containerActionStop)
1996: +	}
1997: +
1998: +	if next.state != running {
1999: +		return actions
2000: +	}
2001: +
2002: +	started := len(actions) != 0 && actions[len(actions)-1] == containerActionStart
2003: +	if !started && previous != nil && previous.health == next.health {
2004: +		return actions
2005: +	}
2006: +	if next.health == clablabruntime.ContainerHealthStarting && !started {
2007: +		return actions
2008: +	}
2009: +
2010: +	return append(actions, containerActionHealthPrefix+next.health)
2011: +}
2012: +
2013: +// containerRemovalActions are the actions Docker reports when a container is removed: a running
2014: +// one is killed and dies first.
2015: +func containerRemovalActions(previous *trackedContainer) []string {
2016: +	if previous.state == clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning {
2017: +		return []string{containerActionKill, containerActionDie, containerActionDestroy}
2018: +	}
2019: +
2020: +	return []string{containerActionDestroy}
2021: +}
2022: +
2023: +// containerActionState is the container state after an action, which every container event
2024: +// reports in its state attribute.
2025: +func containerActionState(action, snapshotState string) string {
2026: +	switch action {
2027: +	case containerActionCreate:
2028: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateCreated
2029: +	case containerActionStop, containerActionDie, containerActionDestroy:
2030: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateExited
2031: +	case containerActionStart, containerActionKill:
2032: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning
2033: +	}
2034: +	if strings.HasPrefix(action, containerActionHealthPrefix) {
2035: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning
2036: +	}
2037: +
2038: +	return snapshotState
2039: +}
2040: +
2041: +func (t *containerTracker) emit(
2042: +	ctx context.Context,
2043: +	state *clablabruntime.LabState,
2044: +	node clablabruntime.NodeState,
2045: +	action string,
2046: +	extra map[string]string,
2047: +) {
2048: +	t.r.sendEvent(ctx, t.sink, c9sContainerEvent(state, node, action, extra))
2049: +}
2050: +
2051: +// c9sContainerEvent is a container event of a node. The actor is the node, as for its interface
2052: +// events; its ID is the container_id inspect reports. Lab and Node let the events command
2053: +// describe the node with the attributes inspect and the Docker runtime report.
2054: +func c9sContainerEvent(
2055: +	state *clablabruntime.LabState,
2056: +	node clablabruntime.NodeState,
2057: +	action string,
2058: +	extra map[string]string,
2059: +) clablabruntime.Event {
2060: +	key := c9sNodeKey(state.Namespace, state.Name, node.Name)
2061: +	attributes := map[string]string{
2062: +		"namespace":                  state.Namespace,
2063: +		"lab":                        state.Name,
2064: +		"node":                       node.Name,
2065: +		containerEventAttributeState: containerActionState(action, node.ContainerState),
2066: +	}
2067: +	if node.Pod != "" {
2068: +		attributes["pod"] = node.Pod
2069: +	}
2070: +	if action == containerActionKill {
2071: +		// Kubernetes stops a pod's containers with SIGTERM.
2072: +		attributes["signal"] = containerEventSignalSIGTERM
2073: +	}
2074: +	for k, v := range extra {
2075: +		attributes[k] = v
2076: +	}
2077: +
2078: +	nodeCopy := node
2079: +
2080: +	return clablabruntime.Event{
2081: +		Timestamp:   time.Now(),
2082: +		Type:        clablabruntime.EventTypeContainer,
2083: +		Action:      action,
2084: +		ActorID:     key,
2085: +		ActorName:   fmt.Sprintf("%s-%s", state.Name, node.Name),

2087: +		Attributes:  attributes,
2088: +		Lab:         state,
2089: +		Node:        &nodeCopy,
2090: +	}

2145: +func interfaceTestSample(
2146: +	node string,
2147: +	ts time.Time,
2148: +	links []clabwireInterface,
2149: +	ifaces []c9sIfaceState,
2150: +) interfaceSample {
2151: +	stats := make([]c9sIfaceStats, 0, len(ifaces))
2152: +	for _, iface := range ifaces {
2153: +		stats = append(stats, c9sIfaceStats{Name: iface.Name, RxBytes: uint64(ts.Unix())})
2154: +	}
2155: +
2156: +	return interfaceSample{
2157: +		target: interfaceSampleTarget{
2158: +			state: &clablabruntime.LabState{Name: "lab1", Namespace: "c9s-lab1"},
2159: +			node: clablabruntime.NodeState{
2160: +				Name: node, Pod: "lab1-" + node + "-abc",
2161: +				ContainerState: clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning,
2162: +			},
2163: +			pod: &corev1.Pod{ObjectMeta: metav1.ObjectMeta{Name: "lab1-" + node + "-abc"}},
2164: +		},
2165: +		stats:     stats,
2166: +		ifaces:    ifaces,
2167: +		hasStates: true,
2168: +		links:     links,
2169: +		timestamp: ts,
2170: +	}
2171: +}

2173: +func interfacesByName(events []clablabruntime.Event) map[string]clablabruntime.Event {
2174: +	byName := map[string]clablabruntime.Event{}
2175: +	for _, ev := range events {
2176: +		byName[ev.Interface.Name] = ev
2177: +	}
2178: +
2179: +	return byName
2180: +}

2201: +// TestInterfaceEventDataNamesInterfacesLikeDocker checks the names, aliases, types and states a
2202: +// Docker container of the same node kind reports: linux eth1 without an alias, SR Linux e1-1
2203: +// with the topology's ethernet-1/1, eth0 and loopback, and no clabernetes alias.
2204: +func TestInterfaceEventDataNamesInterfacesLikeDocker(t *testing.T) {
2205: +	t.Parallel()
2206: +
2207: +	ifaces := map[string]c9sIfaceState{}
2208: +	for _, iface := range podInterfaces("e1-1", "lowerlayerdown") {
2209: +		ifaces[iface.Name] = iface
2210: +	}
2211: +	ifaces["eth1"] = c9sIfaceState{
2212: +		Name: "eth1", Index: 8, MTU: 9500, OperState: "up", Alias: "c9s:direct:v1:x",
2213: +	}
2214: +
2215: +	tests := []struct {
2216: +		iface, alias string
2217: +		want         clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData
2218: +	}{
2219: +		{"eth1", "", clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData{
2220: +			Name: "eth1", Index: 8, MTU: 9500, OperState: "up", Type: "veth",
2221: +		}},
2222: +		{"e1-1", "ethernet-1/1", clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData{
2223: +			Name: "e1-1", Alias: "ethernet-1/1", Index: 7, MTU: 9500, MAC: "fe:0b:87:7a:1d:5a",
2224: +			OperState: "lower-layer-down", Type: "veth",
2225: +		}},
2226: +		{"eth0", "", clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData{
2227: +			Name: "eth0", Index: 4, MTU: 1450, MAC: "3a:3b:86:d8:73:37", OperState: "up",
2228: +			Type: "veth",
2229: +		}},
2230: +		{"lo", "", clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData{
2231: +			Name: "lo", Index: 1, MTU: 65536, OperState: "unknown", Type: "device",
2232: +		}},
2233: +	}
2234: +	for _, tt := range tests {
2235: +		if got := interfaceEventData(ifaces[tt.iface], tt.alias); got != tt.want {
2236: +			t.Fatalf("%s = %+v, want %+v", tt.iface, got, tt.want)
2237: +		}
2238: +	}
2239: +
2240: +	// an alias set inside the node is the interface's alias, as netlink reports it
2241: +	got := interfaceEventData(c9sIfaceState{Name: "eth2", Alias: "uplink"}, "")
2242: +	if got.Alias != "uplink" {
2243: +		t.Fatalf("own alias = %+v", got)
2244: +	}
2245: +}

2247: +// TestInterfacePollerReportsTheTopologyInterfaces runs a linux and an SR Linux node through the
2248: +// poller: only the lab link interfaces (named and aliased as clabwire lists them), eth0 and lo
2249: +// are reported, with the actions of the netlink events.
2250: +func TestInterfacePollerReportsTheTopologyInterfaces(t *testing.T) {
2251: +	t.Parallel()
2252: +
2253: +	tests := []struct {
2254: +		name  string
2255: +		link  string
2256: +		alias string
2257: +	}{
2258: +		{name: "linux", link: "eth1"},
2259: +		{name: "srl", link: "e1-1", alias: "ethernet-1/1"},
2260: +	}
2261: +	for _, tt := range tests {
2262: +		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
2263: +			t.Parallel()
2264: +
2265: +			links := []clabwireInterface{{
2266: +				Node: "r1", Interface: tt.link, Alias: tt.alias,
2267: +				PeerNode: "r2", PeerInterface: tt.link,
2268: +			}}
2269: +			sink := make(chan clablabruntime.Event, 64)
2270: +			p := newInterfacePoller(&Runtime{}, eventScope{},
2271: +				interfacePollOptions{includeInitialState: true}, sink)
2272: +			ctx := context.Background()
2273: +			start := time.Now()
2274: +
2275: +			p.processSample(
2276: +				ctx,
2277: +				interfaceTestSample("r1", start, links, podInterfaces(tt.link, "up")),
2278: +			)
2279: +			got := interfacesByName(drain(sink))
2280: +			if len(got) != 3 {
2281: +				t.Fatalf("first round reported %v, want %s, eth0 and lo", got, tt.link)
2282: +			}
2283: +			link := got[tt.link]
2284: +			if link.Action != interfaceActionSnapshot ||
2285: +				link.Type != clablabruntime.EventTypeInterface ||
2286: +				link.ActorID != "c9s-lab1/lab1/r1" ||
2287: +				link.ActorFullID != "c9s-lab1/lab1/r1" ||
2288: +				link.ActorName != "lab1-r1" || link.Interface.Alias != tt.alias ||
2289: +				link.Interface.OperState != "up" || link.Interface.Type != "veth" ||
2290: +				link.Lab == nil || link.Node == nil || link.Node.Name != "r1" ||
2291: +				link.Attributes["origin"] != "clabernetes" ||
2292: +				link.Attributes["pod"] != "lab1-r1-abc" ||
2293: +				link.Attributes["namespace"] != "c9s-lab1" {
2294: +				t.Fatalf("%s event = %+v", tt.link, link)
2295: +			}
2296: +			if got["lo"].Interface.Type != "device" || got["eth0"].Interface.Alias != "" {
2297: +				t.Fatalf("eth0/lo = %+v %+v", got["eth0"].Interface, got["lo"].Interface)
2298: +			}
2299: +
2300: +			// unchanged: nothing
2301: +			p.firstRound = false
2302: +			p.processSample(ctx, interfaceTestSample("r1", start.Add(time.Second), links,
2303: +				podInterfaces(tt.link, "up")))
2304: +			if events := drain(sink); len(events) != 0 {
2305: +				t.Fatalf("unchanged interfaces reported %+v", events)
2306: +			}
2307: +
2308: +			// the peer goes away: the link loses its carrier
2309: +			p.processSample(ctx, interfaceTestSample("r1", start.Add(2*time.Second), links,
2310: +				podInterfaces(tt.link, "lowerlayerdown")))
2311: +			events := drain(sink)
2312: +			if len(events) != 1 || events[0].Action != interfaceActionUpdate ||
2313: +				events[0].Interface.Name != tt.link ||
2314: +				events[0].Interface.OperState != "lower-layer-down" {
2315: +				t.Fatalf("carrier loss reported %+v", events)
2316: +			}
2317: +
2318: +			// the link interface disappears, then comes back
2319: +			withoutLink := podInterfaces(tt.link, "up")
2320: +			withoutLink = withoutLink[:len(withoutLink)-1]
2321: +			p.processSample(
2322: +				ctx,
2323: +				interfaceTestSample("r1", start.Add(3*time.Second), links, withoutLink),
2324: +			)
2325: +			events = drain(sink)
2326: +			if len(events) != 1 || events[0].Action != interfaceActionDelete ||
2327: +				events[0].Interface.Name != tt.link {
2328: +				t.Fatalf("removed link reported %+v", events)
2329: +			}
2330: +			p.processSample(ctx, interfaceTestSample("r1", start.Add(4*time.Second), links,
2331: +				podInterfaces(tt.link, "up")))
2332: +			events = drain(sink)
2333: +			if len(events) != 1 || events[0].Action != interfaceActionCreate ||
2334: +				events[0].Interface.Alias != tt.alias {
2335: +				t.Fatalf("new link reported %+v", events)
2336: +			}
2337: +
2338: +			// the node stops: its interfaces are deleted
2339: +			p.retireStoppedNodes(ctx, map[string]bool{})
2340: +			got = interfacesByName(drain(sink))
2341: +			if len(got) != 3 || got[tt.link].Action != interfaceActionDelete ||
2342: +				got["eth0"].Action != interfaceActionDelete || len(p.nodes) != 0 {
2343: +				t.Fatalf("stopped node reported %+v", got)
2344: +			}
2345: +		})
2346: +	}
2347: +}

2396: +// listingExec answers clabwire --list-interfaces with the listing of the pod and counts the
2397: +// listings.
2398: +type listingExec struct {
2399: +	mu       sync.Mutex
2400: +	listings map[string][]clabwireInterface
2401: +	calls    int
2402: +}
2403: +
2404: +func (l *listingExec) exec(
2405: +	_ context.Context,
2406: +	_, pod, container string,
2407: +	command []string,
2408: +	_ io.Reader,
2409: +	stdout, _ io.Writer,
2410: +) error {
2411: +	l.mu.Lock()
2412: +	defer l.mu.Unlock()
2413: +
2414: +	if container != clabwireContainer || !slices.Contains(command, "--list-interfaces") {
2415: +		return nil
2416: +	}
2417: +	l.calls++
2418: +
2419: +	return json.NewEncoder(stdout).Encode(l.listings[pod])
2420: +}
2421: +
2422: +func TestInterfacePollerListsAPodOncePerInterfaceSet(t *testing.T) {

2426: +		"lab1-r1-abc": {

2428: +			{Node: "r1b", Interface: "e1-2"},
2429: +		},
2430: +	}}
2431: +	r := &Runtime{podExec: listing.exec}
2432: +	p := newInterfacePoller(
2433: +		r,
2434: +		eventScope{},
2435: +		interfacePollOptions{},
2436: +		make(chan clablabruntime.Event),
2437: +	)
2438: +	target := interfaceTestSample("r1", time.Now(), nil, nil).target
2439: +

2441: +		links, err := p.nodeLinks(context.Background(), target, "e1-1,eth0,lo")
2442: +		if err != nil {
2443: +			t.Fatal(err)
2444: +		}
2445: +		if len(links) != 1 || links[0].Alias != "ethernet-1/1" {
2446: +			t.Fatalf("links = %+v, want the node's own link", links)
2447: +		}
2448: +	}
2449: +	if listing.calls != 1 {
2450: +		t.Fatalf("listed %d times, want once while the interfaces stay the same", listing.calls)
2451: +	}
2452: +
2453: +	if _, err := p.nodeLinks(context.Background(), target, "e1-1,e1-2,eth0,lo"); err != nil {
2454: +		t.Fatal(err)
2455: +	}
2456: +	if listing.calls != 2 {
2457: +		t.Fatalf("listed %d times, want again after the interfaces changed", listing.calls)
2458: +	}
2459: +
2460: +	p.forgetListings(map[string]bool{})
2461: +	if len(p.listings) != 0 {
2462: +		t.Fatalf("listings of gone pods kept: %+v", p.listings)
2463: +	}
2464: +}

2466: +func TestContainerTransitionActions(t *testing.T) {
2467: +	t.Parallel()
2468: +
2469: +	const (
2470: +		created = clablabruntime.ContainerStateCreated
2471: +		running = clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning
2472: +		exited  = clablabruntime.ContainerStateExited
2473: +		healthy = clablabruntime.ContainerHealthHealthy
2474: +		start   = clablabruntime.ContainerHealthStarting
2475: +		sick    = clablabruntime.ContainerHealthUnhealthy
2476: +	)
2477: +	tracked := func(state, health, pod string) *trackedContainer {
2478: +		return &trackedContainer{state: state, health: health, pod: pod}
2479: +	}
2480: +
2481: +	tests := []struct {
2482: +		name     string
2483: +		previous *trackedContainer
2484: +		next     *trackedContainer
2485: +		want     string
2486: +	}{
2487: +		{"new pending node", nil, tracked(created, "", ""), "create"},
2488: +		{
2489: +			"new running node", nil, tracked(running, start, "p1"),
2490: +			"create,start,health_status: starting",
2491: +		},
2492: +		{
2493: +			"new ready node", nil, tracked(running, healthy, "p1"),
2494: +			"create,start,health_status: healthy",
2495: +		},
2496: +		{"new stopped node", nil, tracked(exited, "", ""), "create,stop"},
2497: +		{
2498: +			"pod starts", tracked(created, "", "p1"), tracked(running, start, "p1"),
2499: +			"start,health_status: starting",
2500: +		},
2501: +		{
2502: +			"device ready", tracked(running, start, "p1"), tracked(running, healthy, "p1"),
2503: +			"health_status: healthy",
2504: +		},
2505: +		{"still booting", tracked(running, start, "p1"), tracked(running, start, "p1"), ""},
2506: +		{"no change", tracked(running, healthy, "p1"), tracked(running, healthy, "p1"), ""},
2507: +		{
2508: +			"device not ready again", tracked(running, healthy, "p1"), tracked(running, sick, "p1"),
2509: +			"health_status: unhealthy",
2510: +		},
2511: +		{"stop", tracked(running, healthy, "p1"), tracked(exited, "", ""), "kill,stop,die"},
2512: +		{
2513: +			"start", tracked(exited, "", ""), tracked(running, healthy, "p2"),
2514: +			"start,health_status: healthy",
2515: +		},
2516: +		{
2517: +			"restart, new pod pending", tracked(running, healthy, "p1"), tracked(created, "", "p2"),
2518: +			"kill,stop,die",
2519: +		},
2520: +		{
2521: +			"restart, new pod running", tracked(running, healthy, "p1"),
2522: +			tracked(running, start, "p2"), "kill,stop,die,start,health_status: starting",
2523: +		},
2524: +		{"stopped before it ran", tracked(created, "", "p1"), tracked(exited, "", ""), "stop"},
2525: +		{"stopped node rescheduled", tracked(exited, "", ""), tracked(created, "", "p2"), ""},
2526: +	}
2527: +	for _, tt := range tests {
2528: +		got := strings.Join(containerTransitionActions(tt.previous, tt.next), ",")
2529: +		if got != tt.want {
2530: +			t.Fatalf("%s: actions = %q, want %q", tt.name, got, tt.want)
2531: +		}
2532: +	}
2533: +
2534: +	if got := strings.Join(containerRemovalActions(tracked(running, healthy, "p1")), ","); got !=
2535: +		"kill,die,destroy" {
2536: +		t.Fatalf("removal of a running node = %q", got)
2537: +	}
2538: +	if got := strings.Join(
2539: +		containerRemovalActions(tracked(exited, "", "")),
2540: +		",",
2541: +	); got != "destroy" {
2542: +		t.Fatalf("removal of a stopped node = %q", got)
2543: +	}
2544: +
2545: +	// health: a running node that was healthy and is not ready anymore in the same pod is
2546: +	// unhealthy; in a new pod it starts again
2547: +	previous := tracked(running, healthy, "p1")
2548: +	if got := containerHealth(previous, tracked(running, "", "p1"), start); got != sick {
2549: +		t.Fatalf("health in the same pod = %q", got)
2550: +	}

2666: +// refresh reads lab1 again and returns the reported actions with their state attribute.
2667: +func (h *trackerHarness) refresh() string {

2677: +			ev.ActorFullID != "lab-ns/lab1/r1" || ev.ActorName != "lab1-r1" ||
2678: +			ev.Lab == nil || ev.Node == nil || ev.Node.TopologyName != "R1" ||
2679: +			ev.Attributes["lab"] != "lab1" || ev.Attributes["node"] != "r1" ||
2680: +			ev.Attributes["namespace"] != "lab-ns" {
2681: +			h.t.Fatalf("container event = %+v", ev)
2682: +		}

2685: +			action += "/" + signal
2686: +		}
2687: +		actions = append(actions, action)
2688: +	}
2689: +
2690: +	return strings.Join(actions, ",")
2691: +}

2693: +// TestContainerTrackerReportsTheNodeLifecycle drives one node through boot, readiness, stop,

2695: +func TestContainerTrackerReportsTheNodeLifecycle(t *testing.T) {

2738: +	}

2743: +	)

2746: +	}

\"\n+\tcontainerEventSignalSIGTERM  = \"15\"\n+\tcontainerRefreshDelay        = 200 * time.Millisecond\n+\tcontainerRefreshRetryDelay   = pollInterval\n+\tcontainerEventAttributeState = lab and node are the last observation, which a removed node is described with.\n+\tlab  *clablabruntime.LabState\n+\tnode clablabruntime.NodeState\n+}\n+\n+// containerTracker turns the node states of the labs in an event scope into the container\n+// events the Docker runtime emits for the same lifecycle: create and start when a node's pod\n+// starts, kill, stop and die when it stops (on request, or between the pods of a restart),\n+// destroy when the node is removed, and health_status as its device becomes ready.\n+type containerTracker struct {\n+\tr     *Runtime\n+\tsink  chan<- clablabruntime.Event\n+\tnodes map[string]*trackedContainer\n+}\n+\n+// trackContainers reports the container events of a scope. It reads the labs once, reporting\n+// them as a snapshot when the stream includes the initial state, and then reads a lab again\n+// whenever its Topology, Nodes or pods change. Changes that arrive together are read once.\n+func (r *Runtime) trackContainers(\n+\tctx context.Context,\n+\tscope eventScope,\n+\tincludeInitialState bool,\n+\tchanged <-chan labRef,\n+\teventSink chan<- clablabruntime.Event,\n+\terrSink chan<- error,\n+) {\n+\ttracker := &containerTracker{r: r, sink: eventSink, nodes: map[string]*trackedContainer{}}\n+\n+\tstates, err := r.labStates(ctx, scope)\n+\tif err != nil {\n+\t\tsendEventError(ctx, errSink, err)\n+\t\treturn\n+\t}\n+\tfor _, state := range states {\n+\t\ttracker.observeLab(ctx, labRef{namespace: state.Namespace, lab: state.Name}, state,\n+\t\t\t&observeSeed{snapshot: includeInitialState})\n+\t}\n+\n+\tpending := map[labRef]bool{}\n+\ttimer := time.NewTimer(time.Hour)\n+\ttimer.Stop()\n+\tdefer timer.Stop()\n+\tarmed := false\n+\tarm := func(delay time.Duration) {\n+\t\tif !armed {\n+\t\t\ttimer.Reset(delay)\n+\t\t\tarmed = true\n+\t\t}\n+\t}\n+\n+\tfor {\n+\t\tselect {\n+\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n+\t\t\treturn\n+\t\tcase ref := <-changed:\n+\t\t\tpending[ref] = true\n+\t\t\tarm(containerRefreshDelay)\n+\t\tcase <-timer.C:\n+\t\t\tarmed = false\n+\t\t\trefs := pending\n+\t\t\tpending = map[labRef]bool{}\n+\t\t\tfor ref := range refs {\n+\t\t\t\tif !tracker.refresh(ctx, ref) {\n+\t\t\t\t\tpending[ref] = true\n+\t\t\t\t}\n+\t\t\t}\n+\t\t\tif len(pending) != 0 {\n+\t\t\t\tarm(containerRefreshRetryDelay)\n+\t\t\t}\n+\t\t}\n+\t}\n+}\n+\n+// observeSeed marks the first observation of a stream: it records the node states without\n+// reporting changes, and reports them as a snapshot when asked to.\n+type observeSeed struct {\n+\tsnapshot bool\n+}\n+\n+// refresh reads a lab again and reports what changed for its nodes. A lab that is gone reports\n+// its nodes as destroyed. It returns false when the lab could not be read.\n+func (t *containerTracker) refresh(ctx context.Context, ref labRef) bool {\n+\tstate, err := t.r.Inspect(ctx, clablabruntime.InspectRequest{\n+\t\tName:      ref.lab,\n+\t\tNamespace: ref.namespace,\n+\t})\n+\tif err != nil {\n+\t\tvar notFound *labNotFoundError\n+\t\tif !errors.As(err, &notFound) {\n+\t\t\tif ctx.Err() == nil {\n+\t\t\t\tlog.Debug(\"Failed to read a C9s lab for container events\",\n+\t\t\t\t\t\"namespace\", ref.namespace, \"lab\", ref.lab, \"error\", err)\n+\t\t\t}\n+\n+\t\t\treturn ctx.Err() != nil\n+\t\t}\n+\t\tstate = nil\n+\t}\n+\n+\tt.observeLab(ctx, ref, state, nil)\n+\n+\treturn true\n+}\n+\n+// observeLab compares the nodes of a lab with what was reported before and reports the\n+// difference. A nil state removes every node of the lab.\n+func (t *containerTracker) observeLab(\n+\tctx context.Context,\n+\tref labRef,\n+\tstate *clablabruntime.LabState,\n+\tseed *observeSeed,\n+) {\n+\tseen {\n+\t\t\tcontinue\n+\t\t}\n+\n+\t\tfor _, action := range containerRemovalActions(tracked) {\n+\t\t\tt.emit(ctx, tracked.lab, tracked.node, action, nil)\n+\t\t}\n+\t\tdelete(t.nodes, key)\n+\t}\n+}\n+\n+func (t *containerTracker) observeNode(\n+\tctx context.Context,\n+\tref labRef,\n+\tstate *clablabruntime.LabState,\n+\tnode clablabruntime.NodeState,\n+\tseed *observeSeed,\n+) {\n+\tkey := c9sNodeKey(state.Namespace, state.Name, node.Name)\n+\tprevious := t.nodes[key]\n+\tif node.ContainerState == \"\" {\n+\t\tnode.ContainerState = clablabruntime.ContainerStateCreated\n+\t}\n+\tnext := &trackedContainer{\n+\t\tref:   ref,\n+\t\tstate: node.ContainerState,\n+\t\tpod:   node.Pod,\n+\t\tlab:   state,\n+\t\tnode:  node,\n+\t}\n+\tnext.health = containerHealth(previous, next, node.Health)\n+\tt.nodes[key] = next\n+\n+\tif seed != nil {\n+\t\tif seed.snapshot {\n+\t\t\tt.emit(ctx, state, node, next.state, map[string]string{\n+\t\t\t\t\"origin\": clablabruntime.EventOriginSnapshot,\n+\t\t\t})\n+\t\t}\n+\n+\t\treturn\n+\t}\n+\n+\tfor _, action := range containerTransitionActions(previous, next) {\n+\t\tt.emit(ctx, state, node, action, nil)\n+\t}\n+}\n+\n+// containerHealth is the health a running node reports: a node that was healthy and is not\n+// ready anymore in the same pod is unhealthy, as a container whose healthcheck starts failing.\n+func containerHealth(previous, next *trackedContainer, health string) string {\n+\tif next.state != clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning {\n+\t\treturn \"\"\n+\t}\n+\tif health == \"\" {\n+\t\thealth = clablabruntime.ContainerHealthStarting\n+\t}\n+\tif health == clablabruntime.ContainerHealthStarting && previous != nil &&\n+\t\tprevious.state == clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning && previous.pod == next.pod &&\n+\t\t(previous.health == clablabruntime.ContainerHealthHealthy ||\n+\t\t\tprevious.health == clablabruntime.ContainerHealthUnhealthy) {\n+\t\treturn clablabruntime.ContainerHealthUnhealthy\n+\t}\n+\n+\treturn health\n+}\n+\n+// containerTransitionActions are the container event actions Docker reports when a container\n+// goes from the previous to the next state:\n+//\n+//\tnew node            create (+ start when it runs, + stop when it is already stopped)\n+//\tcreated -> running  start\n+//\texited  -> running  start\n+//\trunning -> exited   kill, stop, die (stop, or the old pod of a restart ending)\n+//\trunning -> created  kill, stop, die (the replacement pod of a restart running && next.state != running:\n+\t\tactions = append(actions, containerActionKill, containerActionStop, containerActionDie)\n+\tcase previousState != running && next.state == 3035: +	newTopology := topologyObject("lab1", "lab-ns", "", "topology:\n")

3039: +		t.Fatalf("Topology file = %q", got)
3040: +	}
3041: +}

3712: +		if err != nil {
3713: +			return nil, err
3714: +		}

3719: +		}

3721: +			listing.byNode[entry.Node] = append(listing.byNode[entry.Node], entry)
3722: +		}

3724: +		p.listingsMu.Lock()

3726: +		p.listingsMu.Unlock()

3756: +// interfaceEventData describes an interface the way the local runtimes' netlink events do: the
3757: +// alias is the topology's name for the interface (an alias clabernetes set for its own use is
3758: +// not reported), the operational state uses netlink's names, lab link and management
3759: +// interfaces are veth pairs, loopback a device, and loopback has no hardware address.
3760: +func interfaceEventData(iface c9sIfaceState, alias string) clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData {
3761: +	if alias == "" && !strings.HasPrefix(iface.Alias, clabernetesAliasPrefix) {
3762: +		alias = iface.Alias
3763: +	}
3764: +
3765: +	mac := iface.MAC
3766: +	if mac == zeroMAC {
3767: +		mac = ""
3768: +	}
3769: +
3770: +	linkType := interfaceTypeVeth
3771: +	if iface.Name == loopbackInterface {
3772: +		linkType = interfaceTypeDevice
3773: +	}
3774: +
3775: +	return clablabruntime.InterfaceEventData{
3776: +		Name:      iface.Name,
3777: +		Alias:     alias,
3778: +		Index:     iface.Index,
3779: +		MTU:       iface.MTU,
3780: +		MAC:       mac,
3781: +		OperState: netlinkOperState(iface.OperState),
3782: +		Type:      linkType,
3783: +	}
3784: +}
3785: +
3786: +// netlinkOperState names an operational state from sysfs (RFC 2863, as in
3787: +// /sys/class/net/<if>/operstate) the way netlink, and so the local runtimes' events, name it.
3788: +func netlinkOperState(operState string) string {
3789: +	switch operState {
3790: +	case "notpresent":
3791: +		return "not-present"
3792: +	case "lowerlayerdown":
3793: +		return "lower-layer-down"
3794: +	case "":
3795: +		return "unknown"
3796: +	default:
3797: +		return operState
3798: +	}
3799: +}

4039: +		TxPackets: stat.TxPackets,
4040: +		RxBps:     float64(counterDelta(stat.RxBytes, previous.Stats.RxBytes)*8) / seconds,
4041: +		TxBps:     float64(counterDelta(stat.TxBytes, previous.Stats.TxBytes)*8) / seconds,
4042: +		RxPps:     float64(counterDelta(stat.RxPackets, previous.Stats.RxPackets)) / seconds,

4502: +// nodeContainerState is the state of a node in container runtime terms, from its pod: created
4503: +// while the pod is scheduled and prepared (init containers), running once the device containers
4504: +// run, and exited once the node was stopped, its pod is being deleted, or the pod ended. Between
4505: +// the pods of a restart the node is exited, then created.
4506: +func nodeContainerState(stopRequested bool, pod *corev1.Pod) string {
4507: +	switch {
4508: +	case pod == nil && stopRequested:
4509: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateExited
4510: +	case pod == nil:
4511: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateCreated
4512: +	case stopRequested || pod.DeletionTimestamp != nil:
4513: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateExited
4514: +	}
4515: +
4516: +	switch pod.Status.Phase {
4517: +	case corev1.PodRunning:
4518: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateRunning
4519: +	case corev1.PodSucceeded, corev1.PodFailed:
4520: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateExited
4521: +	default:
4522: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerStateCreated
4523: +	}
4524: +}

4526: +// nodeHealth is the health of a running node: healthy once the Node is ready or its device
4527: +// containers are, starting before. Device containers are the analogue of a container
4528: +// healthcheck: a node whose links wait for a stopped peer is not ready, but its device is.
4529: +func nodeHealth(node clablabruntime.NodeState, resource *unstructured.Unstructured) string {
4530: +	if node.Ready {
4531: +		return clablabruntime.ContainerHealthHealthy
4532: +	}
4533: +	if resource != nil {
4534: +		if condition := nodeCondition(resource, nodeConditionContainersReady); condition != nil &&
4535: +			condition.status == string(metav1.ConditionTrue) {
4536: +			return clablabruntime.ContainerHealthHealthy
4537: +		}
4538: +	}
4539: +
4540: +	return clablabruntime.ContainerHealthStarting
4541: +}
